The slack data of the recording tableaux in the quantum Littlewood-Richardson map determine its inverse: some applications
Olga Azenhas

Abstract
. We introduce the slack of a recording tableau in the quantum Littlewood-Richardson (LR) map and show that it inherits the needed data from LR-Sundaram tableaux to define the inverse of the quantum LR map. Notably this enriched slack information packs the suitable reverse Schensted column insertion routes to compute the inverse. The slack data is then applied to 𝔨 \mathfrak{k} -highest symplectic tableaux.



2000 Mathematics Subject Classification
05E05, 05E10, 05E14, 17B37, 68Q17

1.
Introduction

This note is a follow-up of the author’s paper
[
Aze26
]
on the recording tableaux of the quantum LR map by Watanabe
[
Wat25b
]
and its role (by analogy of the G. Thomas
[
Tho78
]
LR bijection) on the computation of
\mathfrak{k}
-highest or lowest weight tableaux in a recent
proof of the Naito–Sagaki conjecture via the branching rule for
\imath
quantum groups
[
NSW26
]
.

Our goal with this manuscript is to surface in a conceptual way the dual information hidden on the recording tableaux in the quantum LR map to exhibit the inverse of the quantum LR map. We look at the recording tableaux trough its decomposition into vertical strips (uniquely defined by a quantum LR recording tableau ) and read the gaps in the disconnected vertical strips as dual information to define the inverse of the quantum LR map. It turns out that disconnected vertical strips are the interesting ones and we call to their gaps,
slacks
. We then organize that slack data through slack numbers by counting the gaps, slack vectors defined by the row gap coordinates, and associate incidence slack vectors (see Subsection
3.3
and Definition
2
). Then we show that these tools inherit the needed information of LR Sundaram tableaux via the quantum LR recording tableaux to guarantee the good reverse bumping routes to compute the inverse of the quantum LR map as proposed in
[
Aze26
]
.

We work this in Section
3
. Our main results are Proposition
3
, Theorem
2
and Corollary
1
. In Section
4
, Example
9
, we exhibit several patterns for
\mathfrak{k}
-height weights when
n=3
. The exploration of the case
n=3
reveals interesting properties on the generation of
\mathfrak{k}
-highest weights that should be further studied.

Section
2
collects previous information in
[
Wat25b
]
and
[
Aze26
]
needed for this follow-up.

2.
Preliminaries

We follow the notation in
[
Aze26
]
and
[
Wat25b
]
and in this section we collect needed information from those sources.
A
partition

\gamma
is a weakly decreasing sequence of nonnegative integers
\gamma_{1}\geq\gamma_{2}\geq\cdots

such that
\gamma_{k}=0
for some
k\geq 1
. The maximal
i
such that
\gamma_{i}>0
is called the
number

of
parts
or
length
of
\gamma
, denoted
\ell(\gamma)
. For each
m\geq 0
, the set of partitions of length at most
m
is denoted by
Par_{\leq m}
. We assume the inclusion
Par_{\leq m}\subseteq Par_{\leq k}
whenever
k\geq m
. Thus we often write the partition
\gamma
as a vector

\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\dots,\gamma_{k})
for
k\geq\ell(\gamma)
. The empty partition is the empty sequence
()
and is regarded as the unique partition of length zero. The partition
\gamma
is said to be
even
if
\gamma_{2i-1}=\gamma_{2i}
for all
i\geq 1
. In other words, all columns of
\gamma
have even length and necessarily the length of
\gamma
is even. Given
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
\varpi_{m}
denotes the partition of length
m
whose parts are all
1
, that is,
\varpi_{m}=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{m})=:(1^{m})
.

A partition
\gamma
is identified
with its
Young diagram

D(\gamma)
which is a left and top justified collection of boxes (or cells)
with
\gamma_{k}
many boxes in the
k
th row for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>}0
. In particular, the empty Young diagram and the partition
()
are identified. The number of cells of
D(\gamma)
is the sum of the parts of
\gamma
and is denoted by
|\gamma|
.
The boxes or cells of the Young diagram of
\gamma
are identified by its coordinates
(i,j)
in the matrix style, that is,
1\leq i\leq\ell(\gamma)
and
1\leq j\leq\gamma_{i}
.

Let
\gamma
,
\mu
partitions with
\mu\subseteq\gamma
, that is,
\mu_{i}\leq\gamma_{i}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{>}0
, or the Young diagram of
\mu
is a subset of
the Young diagram of
\gamma
. A

tableau

T
of (skew) shape
\gamma/\mu
is a map (or a filling of
D(\gamma)
)

T:D(\gamma)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq}0,\;(i,j)\mapsto T(i,j),

assigning a positive integer to each box of
\gamma\setminus\mu
and
0
to the boxes corresponding to
\mu
. Denote by
\mathrm{Tab}(\gamma/\mu)
the set of tableaux of shape
\gamma/\mu
. We say that the tableau
T
is
semi-standard
if an addition the assignment is
such that it is weakly increasing as we go from left to right along a row and strictly
increasing as we go from top to bottom along a column excluding the boxes in
\mu
,

\displaystyle T(i,j)\leq T(i,j+1),\;T(i,j)<T(i+1,j),\mbox{ for all $(i,j)\in D(\gamma)\setminus D(\mu)$,}

\displaystyle\mbox{ and }T(i,j)=0,\mbox{ for $(i,j)\in D(\mu)$,}

where we set
T(a,b):=\infty
if
(a,b)\notin D(\gamma)
.
Usually
T(i,j)
is just referred as
the entry in the box
(i,j)
and we omit the zeroes in the boxes of
\mu
. A positive integer

m\geq\ell(\gamma)
will be fixed and
[0,m]:=\{0,1,\dots,m\}
will be used as a co-domain for the map
T
. We call

[m]:=\{1,\dots,m\}
the alphabet of the semi-standard tableau
T
.
In this case, we will denote the
set
of
semi-standard

tableaux
of
shape

\gamma/\mu
by

SST_{k}(\gamma/\mu)
. When
\mu=()
, we just write
SST_{m}(\gamma)
. The
weight
or
content
of
T
is the nonnegative vector
\mathrm{wt}(T)=(T[1],\dots,T[m])
, where
T[i]:=\#\{(a,b)\in D(\gamma):T(a,b)=i\}
for
i\in[m]
, that is,
T[i]
is the number of occurrences of
i
in the tableau
T
.
The
reverse

row

word
of a semi-standard tableau

T
, denoted
w(T)=w_{1}\cdots w_{l}
, with
l
the number of non zero entries of
T
, is obtained by reading the entries of its rows (excluding the entry 0) right to left
starting from the top row and proceeding downwards. The
column

word
of a semi-standard tableau
T
denoted
w^{col}(T)=w^{\prime}_{1}\cdots w_{l}^{\prime}
is the sequence of entries obtained by reading the entries (excluding the entry
0
) of its columns
from bottom to top and left to right.
The
reverse

column

word
of
T
,
w^{\prime}_{l}\cdots w^{\prime}_{1}
.
is obtained by reading the entries of its columns (excluding the entry 0) right to left
starting from the top and proceeding downwards.
The
weight
of the
word

w(T)
is the weight of
T
.

A
Yamanouchi

word
is a word
u_{1}\cdots u_{l}
such
that, for each
1\leq k\leq l
, the weight of the subword
u_{1}\cdots u_{k}
is a partition.
A Yamanouchi tableau of shape
\mu
is a semi-standard tableau of shape
\mu
whose word is Yamanouchi, equivalently
Y(i,j)=i
for all
1\leq i\leq\ell(\mu)
and
1\leq j\leq\mu_{i}
. A Yamanouchi tableau of shape
\mu
is the unique semi-standard tableau whose weight is
\mu
.

For
\lambda\subseteq\gamma
partitions, we write
\lambda\subset_{vert}\gamma
to mean that
\gamma/\lambda
is a
vertical

strip
. The
length
of
\gamma/\lambda
is
|\gamma/\lambda|:=|\gamma|-|\lambda|.

For
\lambda\subseteq\gamma
partitions, we write
\lambda\subset_{vert}\gamma
to mean that
\gamma/\lambda
is a
vertical

strip
. The
size
of
\gamma/\lambda
is
|\gamma/\lambda|:=|\gamma|-|\lambda|
the number of its cells.

2.1.
LR-Sundaram tableaux and orthogonal transposition symmetry

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The set of semistandard Young tableaux of shape
\lambda
with entries in
[1,2n]:=\{1,\dots 2n\}
is denoted by
SST_{2n}(\lambda)
and necessarily
\lambda
has at most
2n
parts,
\lambda\in Par_{\leq 2n}
.

Let
\mu\subseteq\lambda\in Par_{\leq 2n}
. A tableau
T\in SST_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
is said to be a Littlewood-Richardson (LR) tableau
[
LR34
]
if its reverse column word
is a Yamanouchi word. This subset of
SST_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
consisting of LR tableaux is denoted by
LR_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
.
A partition
\nu
is said to be even if all columns of
\nu
have even length and necessarily the length of
\nu
is even. In this case,
\nu^{t}
, the transpose or conjugate of
\nu
, has even parts.
A Littlewood-Richardson-Sundaram tableau or symplectic Littlewood-Richardson tableau
[
Sun86
]
is a certain LR tableau as follows.

Definition 1
.

Let
\lambda\in Par_{\leq 2n}
and
\mu\in Par_{\leq n}
. A semi-standard tableau
T\in SST_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
is said to be
an
n
-symplectic Littlewood-Richardson tableau or Littlewood-Richardson-Sundaram tableau if it satisfies the following:

(1)

The reverse column word of
T

is a Yamanouchi word.

(2)

The sequence
\mathrm{wt}(T)=(T[1],T[2],\dots,T[2n])
is an even partition,

(3)

T(n+i,1)\geq 2i
for every
i\geq 0
.

For
\lambda\in Par_{\leq 2n}
and
\mu\in Par_{\leq n}
, the subset of
SST_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
satisfying conditions
(1)
,
(2)
and
(3)
in Definition
1
is denoted by
LRS_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)\subseteq LR_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
.

Remark 1
.

Let
T\in LRS_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
of content
\nu
. Let
N:=\nu_{1}
and
\nu^{t}=(\nu_{1}^{t},\dots,\nu^{t}_{N})
the transpose of
\nu
. Put
\mu^{(0)}:=\lambda
and
\mu^{(N)}:=\mu
. For
k=1,\dots,N
,
define
\mu^{(k)}
the shape obtained by erasing from NE to SW in
T_{|\mu^{(k-1)}}
,
T
restricted to the shape
\mu^{(k-1)}
, the first rightmost
\nu^{t}_{k}
cells filled with
1,2,\dots,\nu^{t}_{k}
. Hence

\mu^{(0)}=\lambda\supset_{vert}\mu^{(1)}\supset_{vert}\cdots\supset_{vert}\mu^{(N)}=\mu

This decomposes the shape
\lambda/\mu
into
N
vertical strips

J_{1}:=\mu^{(0)}/\mu^{(1)},J_{2}:=\mu^{(1)}/\mu^{(2)},J_{i}=\mu^{(i-1)}/\mu^{i},\dots,J_{N}=\mu^{(N-1)}/\mu^{(N)}

Then, for
k=1,\dots,N
,
the vertical strip
J_{k}=\mu^{(k-1)}/\mu^{(k)}
has
\nu^{t}_{k}=|\mu^{(k-1)}|-|\mu^{(k)}|
cells and is filled in
T
, top to bottom, with

12\cdots\nu_{k}^{t}
.

Concatenating those column words as
12\cdots\nu^{t}_{N}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}\cdots\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}12\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}\cdots\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}\nu^{t}_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}12\cdots\nu^{t}_{1}
gives the reverse-column word of the Yamanouchi tableau of shape
\nu
which is also obtained by rectification of the word of
T
.

Let
T\in LRS_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
of weight
\nu
and
J_{1},\dots,J_{N}
, with
N=\nu_{1}
, its decomposition into vertical strips each strip filled in
1,2,\dots,\nu^{t}_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
for
i=1,\dots,N
.
Relabel each vertical strip
J_{i}=\mu^{(i-1)}/\mu^{(i)}
with
\nu^{t}_{i}

i
’s, for
i=1,\dots,N
. We get a new tableau
Q\in Tab(\lambda/\mu)
of weight
\nu^{t}
. The map on
LRS_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
returning such
Q
is denoted by
\lozenge
and is illustrated below.

Example 1
.

Let
n=3
,
\lambda=(4,3,2,2,1,0)
,
\lambda^{\vee}=(3,2,2,1,0)
,
\mu=(3,1,0)
,
\mu^{\vee}=(4,4,4,3,1)
,
\mu^{t}=(2,1,1,0)
,
\nu=(3,3,1,1,0^{2})
,
\nu^{t}=(4,2,2)
with even parts and
T\in LRS_{6}(\lambda/\mu,\nu)

\displaystyle T=\vbox{\hbox{

}}=\blacklozenge T

\displaystyle\blacklozenge T\in LR({\mu^{\vee}}^{t}/{\lambda^{\vee}}^{t},\nu^{t}).

The bijection
\blacklozenge=\pi\circ\scriptstyle tr\circ\lozenge
is the LR orthogonal symmetry map
[
ACM09
]
.

The tableaux
Q
and
T
are both defined by the sequence of nested partitions

\mu^{(0)}=\lambda\supset_{vert}\mu^{(1)}=(3,2,2,1,0^{2})\supset_{vert}\mu^{(2)}=(3,1,1,0^{3})\supset_{vert}\mu^{(3)}=\mu

which decomposes
\lambda/\mu
into three vertical strips

J_{1}:=\mu^{(0)}/\mu^{(1)},J_{2}:=\mu^{(1)}/\mu^{(2)},J_{3}=\mu^{(2)}/\mu^{(3)}

The bijection
\lozenge
is so natural that we can intertwine LR-Sundaram tableaux with their
Q
presentations.
We define the set

(1)

\displaystyle Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu):=\lozenge LRS(\lambda/\mu)

(2)

\displaystyle LRS_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu).

The set
Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
is characterized in
[
Wat25b
]
(and
[
Aze26
]
) by translating the conditions in Definition
1
to
Q
.

Proposition 1
.

[
Wat25b
]
Let
\lambda\in Par_{\leq 2n}
and
\mu\in Par_{\leq n}
be such that
\mu\subseteq\lambda
. The set
Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)\subseteq Tab(\lambda/\mu)
satisfy the following conditions:

(R1)

The entries of
Q
strictly decrease along the rows from left to right.

(R2)

The entries of
Q
weakly decrease along the columns from top to bottom.

(R3)

For each
k>0
, the number
Q[k]\in 2\mathbb{Z}
.

(R4)

For each
k>0
, it holds that

Q[k]\geq 2(\ell(\mu^{(k-1)})-n),

where
\mu^{(k-1)}
is the partition such that

(3)

\displaystyle D(\mu^{(k-1)})=D(\mu)\cup\{(i,j)\in D(\lambda/\mu)|Q(i,j)\geq k\}.

(R5)

For each
r,k>0
, let
Q_{\leq r}[k]
denote the number of occurrences of
k
in
Q
in the

r
-th row or above. Then, the following inequality holds:

Q_{\leq r}[k+1]\leq Q_{\leq r}[k].

Remark 2
.

Let
N
be the largest entry in
Q
where we are assuming the blank boxes filled with
0
. When
N=0
one has
\lambda=\mu\Leftrightarrow\lambda/\lambda=()\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{wt}(Q)=()
. Let
N\geq 1
.
Since
Q[k]\in 2\mathbb{Z}
and
Q[k+1]\leq Q[k]\leq\ell(\lambda)\leq 2n
, for any
k>0
,
the weight of
Q
is the partition
\mathrm{wt}(Q)=(Q[1],\dots,Q[N])

and its transpose or conjugate is an even partition
\nu=(\nu_{1}=N,\dots,\nu_{Q[1]})
.
Thus

Q
is also defined by the sequence of nested partitions in (
3
)

(4)

\displaystyle\mu^{(0)}=\lambda\supset_{vert}\mu^{(1)}\supset_{vert}\cdots\supset_{vert}\mu^{(N)}=\mu,~~

where
\mu^{(i-1)}/\mu^{(i)}
is a vertical strip of even length
Q[i]\geq 2
satisfying
(R4)
and
(R5)
conditions, for
i=1,\dots,N
.

2.2.
The quantum LR bijection

A semistandard tableau
S\in SST_{2n}(\mu)
is said to
be symplectic if

S(k,1)\geq 2k-1
, for all
k\in[1,\ell(\lambda)]
.
Let
SpT_{2n}(\mu)\subseteq SST_{2n}(\mu)
denote the set of symplectic semi-standard tableaux of shape
\mu
, with entries in
[1,2n]

[
Kin76
,
Wat25b
]
. The definition of symplectic tableau forces that
\mu
has at most
n
parts
[
Aze26
, Proposition 2]
, and in this case
\mu\in Par_{\leq n}
.

Let
\lambda\in Par_{\leq 2n}
. The quantum Littlewood–Richardson bijection of type AII,
LR^{AII}
, is the
algorithm
[
Wat25b
,
Wat25a
, Section 3.1]
which takes
T\in SST_{2n}(\lambda)
as input, and returns a pair of tableaux
(P^{AII}(T),Q^{AII}(T))\in SpT_{2n}(\mu)\times Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
for some
\mu\subseteq\lambda
,

\displaystyle LR^{AII}:

\displaystyle SST_{2n}(\lambda)\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\bigsqcup_{\begin{smallmatrix}\mu\in Par_{\leq n}\\
\mu\subseteq\lambda\end{smallmatrix}}SpT_{2n}(\mu)\times Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu),

(5)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad T\mapsto(P^{AII}(T),Q^{AII}(T))

where
Q^{AII}(T)
is called a recording tableau of skew shape
\lambda/\mu
. In particular, if
\lambda
has at most
n
parts then
SpT_{2n}(\lambda)\subseteq SST_{2n}(\lambda)
and for
T
symplectic,
Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\lambda)=\{()\}
,
LR^{AII}(T)=(P^{AII}(T),Q^{AII}(T))=(T,())
.

Then

(6)

\displaystyle Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)=\{Q^{AII}(T)|T\in SST_{2n}(\lambda)\mbox{ such that $sh(P^{AII}(T))=\mu$}\}

and is called the set of recording tableaux of shape
\lambda/\mu
of
LR^{AII}
.

3.
The algorithm computing the inverse of the quantum LR map

3.1.
Preliminaries on removals

We fix
l\in[0,2n]
and
\mathbf{a}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{l})
a column in
SST_{2n}(\varpi_{l})
.
We often regard
\mathbf{a}
as a set. We recall the following useful property on removals
[
Wat25b
]
in the computation of the inverse reduction.

Proposition 2
.

[
Wat25b
, Proposition 4.2.2, 4.2.7, Corollary 4.2.8]

(1)

i\in[1,l]
and
a_{j}\notin rem(\mathbf{a})
, for
j\in[i,l]
then
rem(\mathbf{a})=rem(a_{1},\dots,a_{i-1})
.

(2)

for each
i\in[1,l]
,
a_{i}\in rem(\mathbf{a})
if and only if one of the following holds

(a)

a_{i}
odd,
i<l
,
a_{i+1}=a_{i}+1
and
a_{i}<2i-|rem(a_{1},\dots,a_{i-1})|

(b)

a_{i}
even,
i>1
,
a_{i}=a_{i-1}+1
and
a_{i}<2i-|rem(a_{1},\dots,a_{i-2})|-1

(3)

a_{i}\in rem(\mathbf{a})
if and only if
s(a_{i})\in rem(\mathbf{a})
. In particular, if for
1<i\leq l
,
(a_{i}-1,a_{i})
with
a_{i}
even, is a removable pair from
\mathbf{a}
, then
a_{i}<2i-1
and
a_{i}
is a symplectic failing entry.
Consequently,
|rem(\mathbf{a})|\in 2\mathbb{Z}
.

3.2.
The algorithm computing the inverse LR map and the row index slack vectors

Let
\mu\subseteq\lambda\in Par_{\leq 2n}
with
\mu\in Par_{\leq n}
. Fix arbitrarily
S\in SpT_{2n}(\mu)
. If
\lambda=\mu
,
Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\lambda)=Rec_{2n}(())
and
{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}(S,())=S
.

(7)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}:SpT_{2n}(\mu)\times Rec_{2n}(())

\displaystyle\longrightarrow SpT_{2n}(\mu)

(8)

\displaystyle(S,())

\displaystyle\mapsto S,~\mathrm{red}(S)=S

Let us consider
\mu\subset\lambda
and define

\displaystyle{LR_{|S}^{AII}}^{-1}:\{S\}\times Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)\longrightarrow SST_{2n}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad(S,Q)\mapsto S^{\mathbf{r}^{(N)}\cdots\mathbf{r}^{(2)}\mathbf{r}^{(1)}}=c\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}^{(1)}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})\circ(\underset{{\mu^{1}/{\mu^{1}}^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}\bigg(\cdots

(9)

\displaystyle(\underset{{\mu^{N-2}/{\mu^{N-2}}^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}\bigg(c\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}^{(N-1)}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})\circ(\underset{{\mu^{N-1}/{\mu^{N-1}}^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}\bigg(c\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}^{(N)}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})\circ(\underset{{\mu^{N}/{\mu^{N}}^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}S)\bigg))\bigg))\cdots)\bigg))

where
N>0
is the number of vertical strips of
Q
,
\lambda=\mu^{0}\supset_{vert}\mu^{1}\supset_{vert}\cdots\supset_{vert}\mu^{N}=\mu
,
[\mathbf{r}^{(N)},\cdots,\mathbf{r}^{(2)},\mathbf{r}^{(1)}]
is the slack vector sequence of
Q
that encodes the slackness of this sequence of vertical strips to be defined in the next two susections,
\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}^{(i)}}^{-1}
is the inverse of the reduction map,
\mathrm{red}
, on
SpT_{2n}(\varpi_{t^{(i)}_{0}})
, or the expanding map via the reverse removal, and
\mathrm{c}
is the concatenation in the plactic monoid. The basic operation

\mathrm{c}\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})\circ(\underset{{\mu/\mu^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}S)

is defined for
N=1
in Theorem
1
in the next subsection.
For the reverse bumping routes properties we refer to
[
Aze26
]
and
[
Ful97
]
.

3.3.
1
-vertical strip recording tableaux, slacks and slack vectors

Let
\mu\subset_{vert}\lambda
with
\ell(\mu)\leq n\leq l=\ell(\lambda)
. Let
t_{0}=\ell(\mu)-l_{0}
where
l_{0}
is the number of cells in the vertical strip
\lambda/\mu
with row coordinates in
[1,\ell(\mu)]
. We call to
t_{0}
the
slack
of the vertical strip
\lambda/\mu
.

Let
\mathbf{r}=\{r_{1}<\dots<r_{t_{0}}\}\subseteq[1,\ell(\mu)]
be the complement of the set of the row coordinates of the cells of the vertical strip
\lambda/\mu
in
[1,\ell(\mu)]
. We call to
\mathbf{r}
the
slack

row

index

vector
of the vertical strip
\lambda/\mu
, and define
\delta_{\mathbf{r}}
to be the
incidence

vector
of the
subset

\mathbf{r}
of
[1,\ell(\mu)]
, that is,
\delta_{\mathbf{r}}=(x_{s})_{s\in[1,\ell(\mu)]}
,
x_{s}=1
if
s\in\mathbf{r}
and
0
otherwise. We call to
\delta_{\mathbf{r}}
the
slack

incidence

vector
of the vertical strip
\lambda/\mu
.

Define
\mu^{\prime}\subset_{vert}\mu
such that
\mu^{\prime}_{i}=\mu_{i}-1
if
i\in\mathbf{r}=\{r_{1},\dots,r_{t_{0}}\}
and
\mu_{i}
, otherwise. That is
\mu^{\prime}=\mu-\delta_{\mathbf{r}}
. Then the vertical strip
\mu/\mu^{\prime}
has
t_{0}=\ell(\mu)-l_{0}
cells with row coordinates
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1}<\dots<r_{t_{0}})
the
slack

row

index vector
of vertical strip
\lambda/\mu
; and

(10)

\displaystyle\lambda=\mu-\delta_{\mathbf{r}}+\varpi_{\ell(\lambda)}=\mu^{\prime}+\varpi_{\ell(\lambda)}\Leftrightarrow\varpi_{\ell(\lambda)}=\lambda-\mu^{\prime}

Let us write
\lambda=(\lambda_{0},\varpi_{l-\ell(\mu)})
where

\mu\subseteq_{vert}\lambda^{0}\in Par_{\leq n}
such that
\ell(\lambda^{0})=\ell(\mu)
and, thus,
\lambda^{0}/\mu
is a vertical strip with
l_{0}=|\lambda_{0}|-|\mu|\leq\ell(\mu)
cells, and slack
t_{0}=\ell(\mu)-l_{0}
.

Let
Q\in Rec_{2n}((\lambda_{0},\varpi_{l-\ell(\mu)})/\mu)
. Then
Q[1]+t_{0}=l
. Furthermore,
the conditions
(R3)
,
(R4)
, in Proposition
1
, impose relations between
l=\ell(\lambda)
and the slack
t_{0}
of
\lambda/\mu
(or
\lambda^{0}/\mu
),

(11)

\displaystyle l\leq 2n-t_{0}\Leftrightarrow l+t_{0}\leq 2n\Leftrightarrow t_{0}\leq 2n-l,

and

(12)

\displaystyle~0\leq l-t_{0}\in 2\mathbb{Z}.

We define the reverse column insertion data of
Q
to be the vertical strip
\mu/\mu^{\prime}
. For simplicity, we often abuse notation and identify the
slack row

index

vector

\mathbf{r}
with
\mu/\mu^{\prime}
and use the slack row index vector
\mathbf{r}
to apply reverse column insertion to an
S\in SpT_{2n}(\mu)
. We also often say the
slack

t_{0}
, the
slack row

index

vector

\mathbf{r}
and the
slack

incidence

vector

\delta_{\mathbf{r}}
of
Q
in the sense that the vertical strip
\lambda/\mu
defines
Q
.

Consequently, the
1
-vertical strip recording tableau of shape
\lambda/\mu
with slack
t_{0}
and
l=\ell(\lambda)
is characterized as

(13)

\displaystyle Rec_{2n}((\lambda_{0},\varpi_{l-\ell(\mu)})/\mu)=\left\{Q=\vbox{\hbox{

Indeed
\varpi_{\ell(\lambda)}=\lambda-\mu^{\prime}
.

Let
S\in SpT_{2n}(\mu)
and
Q\in Rec_{2n}((\lambda_{0},\varpi_{l-\ell(\mu)})/\mu)
.
Let
S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{t_{0}})\in Sp_{2n}(\varpi_{t_{0}})
be the set of the bumped elements from the first column
S_{1}
of
S
by applying successively the reverse column insertion to the rows of
S
, as prescribed by slack row index vector
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1}<\dots<r_{t_{0}})
, going from the largest to the smallest row; and let
S^{\prime}_{\geq 2}
be the remained tableau after the application of that reverse column insertion to
S
.

For the returned pair
(S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}},S^{\prime}_{\geq 2})
obtained under the action of the reverse Schensted insertion to the entries in the cells
\mu/\mu^{\prime}
of
S
, put

(14)

\displaystyle(S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}},S^{\prime}_{\geq 2})=:(\underset{{\mu/\mu^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}S),\mbox{ or $(S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}},S^{\prime}_{\geq 2})=:(\underset{{\mathbf{r}}}{\leftarrow}S)$}

where
S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}\subseteq S_{1}
is the column comprising the
t_{0}
bumped entries from
S_{1}
, and
S^{\prime}_{\geq 2}
is what remains after the bumping applied to the entries in the
t_{0}
cells of
\mu/\mu^{\prime}
in
S
.

When
l_{0}=\ell(\mu)\Leftrightarrow\mathbf{r}=()
,
\mu^{\prime}=\mu
, and the bumped column
S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}=()
is empty,
((),S):=(\underset{{\mu/\mu}}{\leftarrow}S)
.

When
l_{0}=0\Leftrightarrow\mathbf{r}=[1,\ell(\mu)]
,
\mu^{\prime}=\mu-(1^{\ell(\mu)})
and the bumped column
S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}=S_{1}
,
(S_{1},S_{\geq 2}):=(\underset{{\mu/\mu^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}S)
.

Since
S
is symplectic and
S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}
is contained in the first column of
S
,
S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}\subseteq SpT_{2n}(\varpi_{t_{0}})
. (Any subset of a symplectic column is still symplectic.)

For
Q\in Rec_{2n}((\lambda_{0},\varpi_{l-\ell(\mu)})/\mu)
(
13
)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}(S,Q)=\mathrm{c}\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})\circ(\underset{{\mu/\mu^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{c}\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})(S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}},S^{\prime}_{\geq 2})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{c}\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1}(S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}),S^{\prime}_{\geq 2})

(15)

\displaystyle:=S^{\mathbf{r}}

where
\mathrm{c}
means concatenation in the plactic monoid and
\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1}
means
reverse or the inverse

reduction map
applied to a column in
SpT_{2n}(\varpi_{t_{0}})
a symplectic column of length the slack number
t_{0}
of
Q
according to the rules below. The reduction map
\mathrm{red}
on
SST_{2n}(\varpi_{l})
is injective
[
Wat25b
, Proposition 4.3.6, Corollary 4.4.3]
and returns symplectic columns of length
0\leq k\leq\min(l,2n-l)
and
l-k\in 2\mathbb{Z}
. Hence the inverse
\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1}
on
SpT_{2n}(\varpi_{t_{0}})
exists

(16)

\displaystyle\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1}:SpT_{2n}(\varpi_{t_{0}})\rightarrow SST_{2n}(\varpi_{l})

and the rules (
17
), (
18
), (
19
) below exhibit it in (
20
)
[
Aze26
]
.

Let
S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{t_{0}})\in SpT_{2n}(\varpi_{t_{0}})
be the column of bumped entries as in (
14
).
Define
l_{1}
,
l_{2}
,
\dots,l_{t_{0}},l_{t_{0}+1}
nonnegative even numbers such that

(17)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t_{0}}l_{i}+l_{t_{0}+1}=l-t_{0}\Leftrightarrow l_{t_{0}+1}=l-t_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{t_{0}}l_{i}

and

(18)

\displaystyle l_{1}=\begin{cases}a_{1}-2,&\mbox{if }a_{1}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\\
a_{1}-1,&\mbox{if }a_{1}\notin 2\mathbb{Z}\end{cases},

(19)

\displaystyle l_{i+1}=\begin{cases}a_{i+1}-a_{i}-2,&\mbox{if }a_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z},a_{i+1}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\\
&\mbox{ or }a_{i}\notin 2\mathbb{Z},~~a_{i+1}\notin 2\mathbb{Z}\wedge a_{i+1}-a_{i}\geq 2i+1\\
a_{i+1}-a_{i}-1,&\mbox{if }a_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z},a_{i+1}\notin 2\mathbb{Z}\\
&\mbox{ or }a_{i}\notin 2\mathbb{Z},~~~a_{i+1}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\wedge a_{i+1}-a_{i}\geq 2i+1\\
0,&\mbox{ otherwise}\end{cases},1\leq i<t_{0}.

Then

(20)

\displaystyle\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1}(S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}):=T_{0}T_{1}\cdots T_{t_{0}}\in SST_{2n}(\varpi_{l})

where

(21)

\displaystyle T_{0}=(1,2,\dots l_{1}),

(22)

\displaystyle T_{i}=\begin{cases}({a}_{i},a_{i}+1,\dots,a_{i}+l_{i+1}),&\mbox{if }a_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\\
({a}_{i},a_{i}+1+1,a_{i}+1+2,\dots,a_{i}+1+l_{i+1}),&\mbox{if }a_{i}\notin 2\mathbb{Z}\\
\end{cases}

\displaystyle 1\leq i\leq t_{0}.

Theorem 1
.

[
Aze26
]
With the set up above where
S\in SpT_{2n}(\mu)
and
Q\in Rec_{2n}((\lambda^{0},\varpi_{l-\ell(\mu)})/\mu)
,
l=\ell(\lambda)
, has slack row index vector
\mathbf{r}=\{r_{1}<\dots<r_{t_{0}}\}
, one has the following assertion:

\displaystyle{LR^{AII}}^{-1}(S,Q)=c\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})\circ(\underset{{\mu/\mu^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}S)

\displaystyle=c\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})(S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}},S^{\prime}_{\geq 2})

\displaystyle=c\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1}(S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}),S^{\prime}_{\geq 2})

\displaystyle=c\circ(T_{0}T_{1}\cdots T_{t_{0}},S^{\prime}_{\geq 2})

\displaystyle=(T_{0}T_{1}\cdots T_{t_{0}}).S^{\prime}_{\geq 2}

(23)

\displaystyle:=S^{\mathbf{r}}\in SST_{2n}(\lambda)

such that
S^{\prime}_{\geq 2}\in SpT_{2n}(\mu^{\prime})
,
\mu^{\prime}=\mu-\delta_{\mathbf{r}}
and
\lambda=\mu^{\prime}+\varpi_{l}
.

Remark 3
.

If the slack of
Q
is
t_{0}=0
, then
l\in 2\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathrm{red}_{0}^{-1}
means reverse reduction in
[1,l]
applied to a column of length
0
. That is,
\mathrm{red}_{0}^{-1}(())=(12\dots l)
with
l\in 2\mathbb{Z}

\Leftrightarrow red(12\dots l)=()\Leftrightarrow\mathrm{rem}(12\dots l)=(12\dots l)
.

(24)

}}:=V^{\mathbf{r}}\in SST_{12}(\lambda)

3.4.
One or more vertical strips, slack sequences and slack vector sequences

We now introduce the
slack

incidence

matrix
of a recording tableau
Q\in Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
which encodes the information as described in Proposition
3
below to define
{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}
on
SpT_{2n}\times Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
.

Definition 2
.

Let

Q\in Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
defined by the sequence of vertical strips

(27)

\displaystyle\mu^{(0)}=\lambda\supset_{vert}\mu^{(1)}\supset_{vert}\cdots\supset_{vert}\mu^{(N-1)}\supset_{vert}\mu^{(N)}=\mu

such that
2\leq|\mu^{(i-1)}/\mu^{(i)}|=Q[i]\in 2\mathbb{Z}
,
1\leq i\leq N
and
\nu=(Q[1],\dots,Q[N])^{t}
is an even partition. For
1\leq i\leq N
,
let
t_{0}^{(i)}=\ell(\mu^{(i)})-l_{0}^{(i)}
where
l_{0}^{(i)}
is the number of cells in the vertical strip
\mu^{(i-1)}/\mu^{(i)}
with row coordinates in
[1,\ell(\mu^{(i)})]
. Then, for
i=1,\dots,N
,

(1)

t^{(i)}_{0}\in\{0,1,\dots,\ell(\mu^{(i)}\}
is the
slack
of the
vertical

strip

\mu^{(i-1)}/\mu^{(i)}
, and

(2)

\mathbf{r}^{(i)}\subseteq[1,\ell(\mu^{(i)})]
with cardinal
|\mathbf{r}^{(i)}|=t_{0}^{(i)}
,
the corresponding
slack
(
row

index
)
vector
. We write
\mathbf{r}^{(i)}=()
or
\emptyset
when
t_{0}^{(i)}=0
.

The sequence of
slack

numbers

\underline{\mathbf{t}}=(t_{0}^{(N)},\dots,t_{0}^{(1)})
, is called the
slack

sequence
of
Q
uniquely defined by (
27
). Analogously, the corresponding sequence of slack vectors
\underline{\mathbf{r}}=[\mathbf{r}^{(N)},\dots,\mathbf{r}^{(1)}]
is called the
slack
(row index)
vector

sequence
of
Q
.

The
\ell(\mu^{(1)})\times N
matrix
[\delta_{\mathbf{r}^{(N)}},\dots,\delta_{\mathbf{r}^{(1)}}]
is called the
slack

incidence
(row)
matrix
of
Q
.

Example 4
.

In Example
2
,
Q\in Rec_{12}(\lambda/\mu)
,
N=1
,
\mu^{(0)}=\lambda
,
\mu^{(1)}=\mu
and
t_{0}^{(1)}=\ell(\mu)-l^{(1)}_{0}=6-2=4
with
l_{0}^{(1)}=2
, and
Q[1]=4
. Thus
\ell(\mu^{(0)})=\ell(\lambda)=Q[1]+t_{0}^{(1)}=4+4
and
Q[1]+2t_{0}^{(1)}\leq 12
. The slack row index vector is
\mathbf{r}=(1,3,4,6)
, and the
\ell(\mu)\times 1
slack incidence matrix is
[\delta_{\mathbf{r}^{(1)}}]=[1\;0\;1\;1\;0\;1]^{T}

Given
Q\in Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
, Proposition
1
,
(R3)
,
(R4)
impose conditions to the slack sequence of
Q
, and
(R5)
impose conditions to its
slack

vector

sequence

\underline{\mathbf{r}}=[\mathbf{r}^{(N)},\dots,\mathbf{r}^{(1)}]
equivalently to the slack incidence matrix
[\delta_{\mathbf{r}^{(N)}},\dots,\delta_{\mathbf{r}^{(1)}}]
of
Q
.

Given
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}_{\geq}0
,
y\in\mathbb{Z}^{q}_{\geq 0}
, we write
x\leq_{\mathbf{r}}y
to mean
p\geq q\geq 0
, and
x_{i}\leq y_{i}
whenever
y_{i}>0
. In this case,
x\leq_{\mathbf{r}}()
.

Proposition 3
.

Let

Q\in Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
with vertical strip decomposition (
27
), and put
t^{(0)}_{0}:=0
and
\mathbf{r}^{(0)}:=()
. Then, for each
1\leq i\leq N
,

(a)

\ell(\mu^{(i-1)})=Q[i]+t_{0}^{(i)}\geq\ell(\mu^{(i)}
,

(b)

0\leq t^{(i-1)}_{0}\leq t^{(i)}_{0}\leq\ell(\mu^{(i)})\leq\ell(\mu^{(i-1)})
. The slack sequence
\underline{\mathbf{t}}=(t^{(N)}_{0}\geq\dots\geq t^{(1)}_{0})
is weakly decreasing while the sequence
(\ell(\mu^{(N)})\leq\dots\leq\ell(\mu^{(1)})\leq\ell(\mu^{(0)}))
is weakly increasing.

(c)

2n\geq Q[i]+2t_{0}^{(i)}=\ell(\mu^{(i-1)})+t_{0}^{(i)}\Leftrightarrow 2n-t_{0}^{(i)}\geq Q[i]+t_{0}^{(i)}=\ell(\mu^{(i-1)})
.

(d)

\mathbf{r}^{(i)}\leq_{\mathbf{r}}\mathbf{r}^{(i-1)}
. The slack vector sequence
\underline{\mathbf{r}}=(\mathbf{r}^{(N)}\leq_{\mathbf{r}}\cdots\leq_{r}\mathbf{r}^{(1)})
is weakly increasing.

(e)

\mathbf{r}^{(i)}\subseteq[1,\ell(\mu^{(i)}]\subseteq[1,\ell(\mu^{(i-1)}]\subseteq[1,2n-t_{0}^{(i)}]
.

Proof.

(\mathrm{d})
follows from Definition
2
and
(\mathrm{a}),(\mathrm{b}),(\mathrm{c})
.
∎

Notably, condition
(\mathrm{d})
above guarantees that reverse Schensted column insertion can correctly be
\mathbf{r}
-iterated to define
{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}
as required in Remark
.

Remark 4
.

(1)

When the slack sequence of
Q
is the null vector then the conditions on
Q
are just
\ell(\mu^{(i-1)})=Q[i]\geq\ell(\mu^{(i)})
and

2n\geq Q[i]
, for
i=1,\dots,N
. Therefore,
\lambda=\mu+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varpi_{Q[i]}
.

(2)

The admissible slacks for
n=1,2,3
are as follows. Let
N\geq 1
. Proposition
3

(b)
forces
t_{0}^{(i)}=0\Rightarrow t_{0}^{(j)}=0
for all
i\geq j
.

For
n=1
, Proposition
3

(c)
implies
2\geq Q[i]+2t_{0}^{(i)}\geq 2+2t_{0}^{(i)}\Leftrightarrow t_{0}^{(i)}=0
. Hence
Q[i]=2
and
t_{0}^{(i)}=0
.

For
n=2
, Proposition
3

(c)
implies
4\geq Q[i]+2t_{0}^{(i)}\geq 2+2t_{0}^{(i)}\Leftrightarrow 2\geq 2t_{0}^{(i)}\Leftrightarrow 1\geq t_{0}^{(i)}
.
Hence either
Q[i]=4
and
t_{0}^{(i)}=0
or
Q[i]=2
and
t_{0}^{(i)}=0,1
. Note,
t_{0}^{(i)}=1\Rightarrow 2\times 2-1\geq Q[i]+1\Rightarrow Q[i]=2
. In particular
4\notin\mathbf{r}^{(i)}
.

For
n=3
, Proposition
3

(c)
implies
6\geq Q[i]+2t_{0}^{(i)}\geq 2+2t_{0}^{(i)}\Leftrightarrow 2\geq t_{0}^{(i)}
.
Hence either
Q[i]=6
and
t_{0}^{(i)}=0
or
Q[i]=4
and
t_{0}^{(i)}=0,1
or
Q[i]=2
and
t_{0}^{(i)}=0,1,2
. In particular
6\notin\mathbf{r}^{(i)}
.

The following considers the case where no bumping is needed, that is, the slack sequence is the null vector,
\underline{\mathbf{t}}=(t^{(N)}_{0}\geq\dots\geq t^{(1)}_{0})=(0,\dots,0)
. When the slack number is
0
the corresponding slack vector is written
\mathbf{r}=()
. In this case, then
slack
vector
sequence is written
\underline{\mathbf{r}}=((),\dots,())
. It follows from Proposition
3
and the iteration of Remark
3
.

Lemma 1
.

[
Aze26
]
Let
S\in SpT_{2n}(\mu)
and let
Q\in Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
such that
\lambda=\mu+\sum_{i=1}^{\nu_{1}}\varpi_{Q[i]}

with
Q[1]\geq\cdots\geq Q[N]\geq\ell(\mu)
and
\nu=(Q[1],\dots,Q[N])^{t}
an even partition.

Then

(28)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}(S,Q)=Y(\nu)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}S\in SST_{2n}(\lambda)

where
Y(\nu)
is the Yamanouchi tableau of shape
\nu
.

Next one considers constant slack sequences
\underline{\mathbf{t}}=(t^{(N)}_{0}\geq\dots\geq t^{(1)}_{0})=(1,1,\dots,1,0,\dots,0)
possibly with a tail of zeroes. Henceforth, the slack vector sequence
\underline{\mathbf{r}}
is an increasing sequence of numbers possibly with a corresponding tail of emptysets.
From Theorem
1
, (
1
), Proposition
3
, Lemma
1
and Remark 1 in
[
Aze26
]
one has.

Lemma 2
.

[
Aze26
]
Let
S\in SpT_{2n}(\mu)
and let

Q\in Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
defined by the sequence of vertical strips

(29)

\displaystyle\mu^{(0)}=\lambda\supset_{vert}\mu^{(1)}\supset_{vert}\cdots\supset_{vert}\mu^{(N-1)}\supset_{vert}\mu^{(N)}=\mu

such that
|\mu^{(i-1)}/\mu^{(i)}|=Q[i]\geq 2
and
\ell(\mu^{(i-1)})=Q[i]+1
,
1\leq i\leq N
and
(Q[1],\dots,Q[N])^{t}
is an even partition.
For
i=1,\dots,N
, let
\mathbf{r}_{i}\in[1,\ell(\mu^{(i)})]
be the sole row index of
\mu^{(i)}
not a cell row coordinate of the vertical strip
\mu^{(i-1)}/\mu^{(i)}
, or
\mathbf{r}_{i}=()
. Then,

(1)

for
1\leq i\leq N
,

(i)

2n-1\geq Q[i]+1=\ell(\mu^{(i-1)})
, whenever
\mathbf{r}_{i}\neq()
.

(ii)

\mathbf{r}_{N}\leq\cdots\leq\mathbf{r}_{1}
,
1\leq\mathbf{r}_{i}\leq\ell(\mu^{(i)})
,
1\leq i\leq N
.
and
\mathbf{r}_{i}\leq 2n-1
whenever
\mathbf{r}_{i}\neq()
. (Here, for simplicity, since
\mathbf{r}_{i}\neq()
is a single set we abuse notation and identify
\mathbf{r}_{i}
with its single element.)

(2)

(30)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}(S,Q)=S^{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}=S^{\mathbf{r}_{N}\cdots\mathbf{r}_{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}}:={({(\cdots{((S^{\mathbf{r}_{N}})}^{\mathbf{r}_{N-1}}){\cdots})}^{\mathbf{r}_{2}})}^{\mathbf{r}_{1}}.

More generally from Theorem
1
, (
1
), Proposition
3
, Lemma
1
, Lemma
2
and Remark 1 in
[
Aze26
]
.

Theorem 2
.

Let
S\in SpT_{2n}(\mu)
and let

Q\in Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
defined by the sequence of vertical strips

(31)

\displaystyle\mu^{(0)}=\lambda\supset_{vert}\mu^{(1)}\supset_{vert}\cdots\supset_{vert}\mu^{(N-1)}\supset_{vert}\mu^{(N)}=\mu

with slack sequence
\underline{\mathbf{t}}=(t^{(N)}_{0}\geq\dots\geq t^{(1)}_{0})
such that
|\mu^{(i-1)}/\mu^{(i)}|=Q[i]\geq 2
and
\ell(\mu^{(i-1)})=Q[i]+t_{0}^{(i)}
,
1\leq i\leq N
and
(Q[1],\dots,Q[N])^{t}
is an even partition. Let
\underline{\mathbf{r}}=[\mathbf{r}^{(N)},\dots,\mathbf{r}^{(1)}]
be the
slack (row index) vector sequence of
Q
.
Then

(1)

for
1\leq i\leq N
,

(i)

\mathbf{r}^{(N)}\leq_{\mathbf{r}}\cdots\leq_{\mathbf{r}}\mathbf{r}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{r}^{(i)}\subseteq[1,\ell(\mu^{(i)}]\subseteq[1,\ell(\mu^{(i-1)}]\subseteq[1,2n-t_{0}^{(i)}]
.

(ii)

2n-t_{0}^{(i)}\geq Q[i]+t_{0}^{(i)}=\ell(\mu^{(i-1)}
.

(2)

(32)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}(S,Q)=S^{\underline{\mathbf{r}}}=S^{\mathbf{r}^{(N)}\cdots\mathbf{r}^{(2)}\mathbf{r}^{(1)}}:={\bigg({\bigg(\cdots{\big(\big(S^{\mathbf{r}^{(N)}}\big)}^{\mathbf{r}^{(N-1)}}\big)\cdots\bigg)}^{\mathbf{r}^{(2)}}\bigg)}^{\mathbf{r}^{(1)}}.

4.
The inverse quantum LR map as a way to compute
\mathfrak{k}
-pairs

We now apply the explicit inverse of the quantum Littlewood-Richardson map to compute
\mathfrak{k}
-highest or lowest weight tableaux in the
proof of the Naito–Sagaki conjecture via the branching rule for
\imath
quantum groups
[
NSW26
]
.
We closely follow the notation in
[
NSW26
]
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and let us consider the two sequences of positive integers defined in
[
NSW26
]

(33)

\displaystyle u_{k}=2k-\frac{1+(-1)^{k}}{2}

(34)

\displaystyle v_{k}=2k-\frac{1+(-1)^{k+1}}{2},~~k=1,\dots,n.

These two sequences
\{u_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\subseteq[1,2n]
and
\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\subseteq[1,2n]
have no common values and its union gives
\{u_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\sqcup\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}=[1,2n]
.

Example 6
.

For
n=6
,
u_{i}=2,3,6,7,10,11
and
v_{i}=1,4,5,8,9,12
,
1\leq i\leq 6
, and
\{u_{i}\}_{i=1}^{6}\cup\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{6}=\{1,2,\dots,12\}
; and for
n=7
,
u_{i}=2,3,6,7,10,11,14
and
v_{i}=1,4,5,8,9,12,13
,
1\leq i\leq 7
,
and
\{u_{i}\}_{i=1}^{6}\cup\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{6}=\{1,2,\dots,14\}
.

For
S\in SST_{2n}(\lambda)
, its
\mathfrak{k}
-weight is
wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S)=(S[u_{1}]-S[v_{1}],S[u_{2}]-S[v_{2}],\dots,S[u_{n}]-S[v_{n}])
.

For example for
n=3
,
wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S)=(S[2]-S[1],S[3]-S[4],S[6]-S[5])
.

If
S
is the symplectic tableau below on the LHS (
35
) then the shape is equal to the corresponding
\mathfrak{k}
-weight,

wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S)=(S[u_{1}],S[u_{2}],\dots,S[u_{n}])
, and if
S
is on the RHS (
35
) then the
\mathfrak{k}
-weight is
(-S[v_{1}],-S[v_{2}],\dots,-S[v_{n}])
and the shape is

w_{0}wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S)=w_{0}(-S[v_{1}],-S[v_{2}],\dots,-S[v_{n}])=(S[v_{1}],S[v_{2}],\dots,S[v_{n}])

where
w_{0}
is the longest element of the Weyl group of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{k}
isomorphic to the symplectic Lie algebra
\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
,

(35)

u_{1}
u_{1}
\cdots
\cdots
\cdots
\cdots
u_{1}
u_{2}
\cdots
\cdots
\cdots
\cdots
u_{2}
⋮
\vdots
\vdots
⋮
u_{n}
\cdots
u_{n}

v_{1}
v_{1}
\cdots
\cdots
\cdots
v_{1}
v_{2}
\cdots
\cdots
\cdots
v_{2}
⋮
\vdots
\vdots
⋮
v_{n}
\cdots
v_{n}

symplectic
\mathfrak{k}
-highest weight tableau

symplectic
\mathfrak{k}
-lowest weight tableau

Let
SST_{2n}
denote the set of all semi-standard tableaux in the alphabet
[1,2n]
and
SpT_{2n}
its subset of symplectic tableaux.
Next,
\mathfrak{k}
-highest weight tableaux and
\mathfrak{k}
-lowest weight tableaux in
SST_{2n}
are defined.

Definition 3
.

[
NSW26
]
Let
S\in SST_{2n}
.

(1)

S
is called a
\mathfrak{k}
-highest weight tableau if
P^{AII}(S)
is a symplectic tableau of the form shown in the LHS of (
35
); let
SST^{\mathfrak{k}-hw}_{2n}(\lambda)\subseteq SST_{2n}(\lambda)
denote the set of all
\mathfrak{k}
-highest weight tableaux of shape
\lambda
.

(2)

S
is called a
\mathfrak{k}
-lowest weight tableau if
P^{AII}(S)
is a symplectic tableau of the form shown in RHS of (
35
); let
SST^{\mathfrak{k}-lw}_{2n}(\lambda)\subseteq SST_{2n}(\lambda)
denote the set of all
\mathfrak{k}
-lowest weight tableaux of shape
\lambda
.

Since
P^{AII}(S)=S
for
S\in SpT_{2n}
, this definition for
S\in SpT_{2n}
obviously restricts to (
35
).
For
n=1,2,3
, the symplectic
\mathfrak{k}
-highest weight respectively
\mathfrak{k}
-lowest weight tableaux are then respectively of the form

(36)

\displaystyle n=1:~~

\displaystyle S^{H}=\vbox{\hbox{
\hbox to101.58pt{\vbox to15.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-15.06pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

}}\in SpT_{6}

It would be interesting to have an explicit characterization of
SST^{\mathfrak{k}-hw}_{2n}(\lambda)
or
SST^{\mathfrak{k}-lw}_{2n}(\lambda)
. This is known for
n=1,2

[
NSW26
]
. One has an algorithm provided by our results to compute the elements of those sets.

From the quantum Littlewood-Richardson bijection (
2.2
), we see that for each
\mu\in Par_{\leq n}
, the recording tableaux
Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}LRS_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
determine the pairs consisting of
\mathfrak{k}
-highest respectively
\mathfrak{k}
-lowest weight tableaux
S^{H,\mu}
and
S_{L,-\mu}\in SST_{2n}(\lambda)
where
wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S^{H,\mu})=\mu
respectively
wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S_{L,-\mu})=-\mu
. That is, the set
Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
determine the tableaux in

SST^{\mathfrak{k}-hw}_{2n}(\lambda)
respectively
SST^{\mathfrak{k}-lw}_{2n}(\lambda)
) such that
wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(T)=\mu
and respectively
wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(T)=-\mu
. Consequently, our algorithm showing explicitly the surjectivity of
LR^{AII}
in (
3.2
) provides an algorithm for
{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}
to compute those sets.

Proposition 4
.

Let
S^{H,\mu},S_{L,-\mu}\in SpT_{2n}(\mu)
be the symplectic
\mathfrak{k}-hw
respectively
\mathfrak{k}-lw
weight tableaux of
SpT_{2n}(\mu)
. Then

(1)

{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}(\{S^{H,\mu}\}\times Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu))=\{S\in SST^{\mathfrak{k}-hw}_{2n}(\lambda)|wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S)=\mu\}\subseteq SST_{2n}(\lambda)
.

(2)

{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}(\{S_{L,-\mu}\}\times Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu))=\{S\in SST^{\mathfrak{k}-lw}_{2n}(\lambda)|wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S)=-\mu\}\subseteq SST_{2n}(\lambda)
.

Therefore

(39)

\displaystyle SST^{\mathfrak{k}-hw}_{2n}(\lambda)=\bigsqcup_{\begin{smallmatrix}\mu\in Par_{\leq n}\\
\mu\subseteq\lambda\end{smallmatrix}}{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}(\{S^{H,\mu}\}\times Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu))

and

(40)

\displaystyle SST^{\mathfrak{k}-lw}_{2n}(\lambda)=\bigsqcup_{\begin{smallmatrix}\mu\in Par_{\leq n}\\
\mu\subseteq\lambda\end{smallmatrix}}{\mathsf{LR}^{AII}}^{-1}(\{S_{L,-\mu}\}\times Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu))

Let
\mu\subseteq_{vert}\lambda
,
t_{0}
the slack of the vertical strip
\lambda/\mu
and
\mathbf{r}
the corresponding slack row index vector. Let
\mu^{\prime}=\mu-\delta_{\mathbf{r}}
and note
\lambda=\mu^{\prime}+\varpi_{\ell(\lambda)}

For
S
the
\mathfrak{k}
-highest or lowest weight tableau
in
SpT_{2n}(\mu)
, Theorem
1
has a nice assertion.

Corollary 1
.

Let
S^{H,\mu}
be the
\mathfrak{k}
-highest or -lowest weight tableau
S_{L,\mu}
in
SpT_{2n}(\mu)
as in (
37
).
Let
Q\in Rec_{2n}(\lambda/\mu)
with slack row index vector
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{t_{0}})
. Let
u_{\mathbf{r}}=(u_{r_{1}},\dots,u_{r_{t_{0}}})
and
v_{\mathbf{r}}=(v_{r_{1}},\dots,v_{r_{t_{0}}})
sequence of positive numbers in [1,2n] as in (
33
).
Then,

(1)

{LR^{AII}}^{-1}(S^{H,\mu},Q)
returns the following

\mathfrak{k}
-highest weight tableau in

SST_{2n}(\lambda)
with
{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight
\mu
:

(41)

\displaystyle(T^{u}_{0}T^{u}_{1}\cdots T^{u}_{t_{0}})S^{H,\mu^{\prime}}\in SST_{2n}(\lambda)

where
S^{H,\mu^{\prime}}
is the
\mathfrak{k}
-highest in
SpT_{2n}(\mu^{\prime})

and
(T^{u}_{0}T^{u}_{1}\cdots T^{u}_{t_{0}})\in SST_{2n}(\varpi_{\ell(\lambda)})
, given by

\displaystyle T^{u}_{0}=(1,2,\dots l_{1}),

(42)

\displaystyle T^{u}_{i}=\begin{cases}(u_{{r}_{i}},u_{{r}_{i}}+1,\dots,u_{{r}_{i}}+l_{i+1}),&\mbox{if }u_{{r}_{i}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\\
(u_{{r}_{i}},u_{{r}_{i}}+1+1,u_{{r}_{i}}+1+2,\dots,u_{{r}_{i}}+1+l_{i+1}),&\mbox{if }u_{{r}_{i}}\notin 2\mathbb{Z}\\
\end{cases}

\displaystyle 1\leq i\leq t_{0},

with
l_{1},\dots,l_{t_{0}+1}
as in
(
17
), (
18
).

(2)

{LR^{AII}}^{-1}(S_{L,\mu},Q)
returns the

\mathfrak{k}
-lowest weight tableau in

SST_{2n}(\lambda)
with
{\mathfrak{k}}
-weight
-\mu
:

(43)

\displaystyle(T^{v}_{0}T^{v}_{1}\cdots T^{v}_{t_{0}})S_{L,\mu^{\prime}}\in SST_{2n}(\lambda)

where
S_{L,\mu^{\prime}}
is the
\mathfrak{k}
-lowest weight tableau in
SpT_{2n}(\mu^{\prime})

and
(T^{v}_{0}T_{1}\cdots T^{v}_{t_{0}})\in SST_{2n}(\varpi_{\ell(\lambda)})
, given by

\displaystyle T^{v}_{0}=(1,2,\dots l_{1}),

(44)

\displaystyle T^{v}_{i}=\begin{cases}(v_{{r}_{i}},v_{{r}_{i}}+1,\dots,v_{{r}_{i}}+l_{i+1}),&\mbox{if }v_{{r}_{i}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\\
(v_{{r}_{i}},v_{{r}_{i}}+1+1,v_{{r}_{i}}+1+2,\dots,v_{{r}_{i}}+1+l_{i+1}),&\mbox{if }v_{{r}_{i}}\notin 2\mathbb{Z}\\
\end{cases}

\displaystyle 1\leq i\leq t_{0},

with
l_{1},\dots,l_{t_{0}+1}
as in
(
17
), (
18
).

Proof.

Note
\mu^{\prime}=\mu-\delta_{\mathbf{r}}\Leftrightarrow\mu=\mu^{\prime}+\delta_{\mathbf{r}},
and
S^{\prime}_{\mathbf{r}}=u_{\mathbf{r}}\in SpT_{2n}(\varpi_{t_{0}})
and
\lambda=\mu^{\prime}+\varpi_{l}
. Then following the rules in Theorem
1
, one has

\displaystyle{LR^{AII}}^{-1}(S^{H,\mu},Q)=c\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})\circ(\underset{{\mu/\mu^{\prime}}}{\leftarrow}S^{H,\mu})

\displaystyle=c\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1},\mathrm{id})(u_{\mathbf{r}},S^{H,\mu^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=c\circ(\mathrm{red}_{t_{0}}^{-1}(u_{\mathbf{r}}),S^{H,\mu^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=c\circ(T^{u}_{0}T^{u}_{1}\cdots T^{u}_{t_{0}},S^{H,\mu^{\prime}})

(45)

\displaystyle=(T^{u}_{0}T^{u}_{1}\cdots T^{u}_{t_{0}})S^{H,\mu^{\prime}}\in SST_{2n}(\lambda)

The proof for
S_{L,\mu}
is similar.
∎

This result is illustrated for
n=6
in Example
3
.

Remark 5
.

Given
\mu\subseteq_{vert}\lambda
, the data given by the slack number
t_{0}
and the slack row index vector
\mathbf{r}
of
\lambda/\mu
completely characterizes the pair of
\mathfrak{k}
-highest, -lowest weight tableaux in
SST_{2n}(\lambda)
with
\mathfrak{k}
- weights
\mu
, -
\mu
).
That is, for
\mu\subseteq_{vert}\lambda
,
S\in SST^{\mathfrak{k}-hw}_{2n}(\lambda)
(
SST^{\mathfrak{k}-lw}_{2n}(\lambda)
) with
wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S)=\mu
(
wt_{\mathfrak{k}}(S)=-\mu
) if and only if
S=
(
41
) (
=
(
43
)).

The procedure is very easy: given
S^{H,\mu^{\prime}}\in SpT_{2n}(\mu^{\prime})
and
0\leq k\leq n
choose a subset
u=(u_{r_{1}},\dots,u_{r_{k}})\subseteq\{u_{i}\}_{k=1}^{n}
(
33
) such that
\mu=\mu^{\prime}+\delta_{\mathbf{r}}\in Par_{\leq n}
with
\mathbf{r}=({r_{1}},\dots,{r_{k}})\subseteq[1,n]
. Then
u=(u_{r_{1}},\dots,u_{r_{k}})\in SpT_{2n}(\varpi_{k})
and

(46)

\displaystyle\mathrm{red}_{k}^{-1}(u).S^{H,\mu^{\prime}}\in SST^{\mathfrak{k}-hw}_{2n}(\lambda)\mbox{ with $\mathfrak{k}$-weight $\mu$ }

such that
\lambda=\mu^{\prime}+\varpi_{l}
where
k\leq l\leq 2n,~2n-l
and
l-k\in 2\mathbb{Z}
.

Corollary 3
.

Let
n=2
. Consider
S^{H}
the
\mathfrak{k}
-highest weight tableau
in

as in LHS of (

). Let

.

(1)

Q
are of the form
(1,\dots,1,0,\dots,0)
, respectively

(1,\cdots,1,2,\cdots,2,3,\cdots,3,(),\dots,())
, and

(2)

returns a

-highest weight tableau in

with

as described in

, Lemma 6.2,(c)]
:

Example 9
.

For
n=3
, one has

\mathrm{red}_{1}^{-1}(2)=(23456)
,
\mathrm{red}_{1}^{-1}(3)=(12356)
,
\mathrm{red}_{1}^{-1}(6)=(12346)
,
\mathrm{red}_{1}^{-1}(())=(1,2,3,4,5,6)
. Our
\mathfrak{k}
-highest weight tableaux below comprise these columns and the symplectic columns
(2)
,
(2,3)
,
2,3,6
and the columns obtained either with the operations

(\underset{5}{\leftarrow}(2,3,4,5,6))=(6,(1,2,3,4,5))=(1,2,3,4,6).(2,3,4,5)

(\underset{5}{\leftarrow}(1,2,3,5,6))=(6,(1,2,3,5))=(1,2,3,4,6).(1,2,3,5)

(\underset{5}{\leftarrow}(1,2,3,4,6))=(6,(1,2,3,4))=(1,2,3,4,6).(1,2,3,4)

These are the columns that appear in the patterns below

(1)

We expand slack vector the following illustration in
[
Aze26
]
revealing other patterns as expected. The slack sequence is constant equal to
1
and
()
.

After this the type stabilizes in this form. We draw the attention of the reader for the three first rows of this pattern, entry
6
apart, which correspond to (
50
).

(2)

Another admissible pattern are the ones produced by slack sequences with entries equal to two and tails of
1
’s and
()
as in Example
5
.

The exploration of the case
n=3
reveals interesting properties of the generation of
\mathfrak{k}
-highest weights that should be further studied. The dual information of a recording tableau packed on the slack information is ana attempt to get insight.
